**Assistant** (2026-04-29 12:17:59):

Refactored the app into smaller, cleaner pieces and added a couple of practical features without disturbing the existing tax behavior. `MainActivity` is now just the entry point in [MainActivity.kt](/Users/marufalam/AndroidStudioProjects/BDTaxCalculator/app/src/main/java/com/maruf/bdtaxcalculator/MainActivity.kt), the tax rules/models/formatters live under [tax](/Users/marufalam/AndroidStudioProjects/BDTaxCalculator/app/src/main/java/com/maruf/bdtaxcalculator/tax), and the Compose UI is grouped in [TaxCalculatorScreen.kt](/Users/marufalam/AndroidStudioProjects/BDTaxCalculator/app/src/main/java/com/maruf/bdtaxcalculator/ui/screen/TaxCalculatorScreen.kt). I also moved the app back onto the shared theme layer and updated it with the calculator’s green palette in [Theme.kt](/Users/marufalam/AndroidStudioProjects/BDTaxCalculator/app/src/main/java/com/maruf/bdtaxcalculator/ui/theme/Theme.kt) and [Color.kt](/Users/marufalam/AndroidStudioProjects/BDTaxCalculator/app/src/main/java/com/maruf/bdtaxcalculator/ui/theme/Color.kt).

On the feature side, the screen now preserves entered values across recreation, includes a reset action, shows a quick tax summary card, and improves the investment recommendation by showing remaining rebate opportunity. I also added calculation tests in [TaxCalculatorTest.kt](/Users/marufalam/AndroidStudioProjects/BDTaxCalculator/app/src/test/java/com/maruf/bdtaxcalculator/TaxCalculatorTest.kt) so future rule changes are safer.

Verification passed with `./gradlew testDebugUnitTest` and `./gradlew assembleDebug`.

There were already unrelated uncommitted changes in the repo before I started, including launcher assets, Gradle files, and some IDE files, so I left those in place and worked around them. If you want, the next step can be adding a proper `ViewModel` layer and export/share features for the tax report.

**Assistant** (2026-04-29 12:44:45):

Yes, this feature can live inside the app with the privacy constraints you listed, and I’ve added it that way.

The app now includes an offline audit checker section built from a bundled static asset at [audit.json](/Users/marufalam/AndroidStudioProjects/BDTaxCalculator/app/src/main/assets/audit.json). The loader in [AuditRepository.kt](/Users/marufalam/AndroidStudioProjects/BDTaxCalculator/app/src/main/java/com/maruf/bdtaxcalculator/audit/AuditRepository.kt) reads the JSON once from assets, keeps it only in memory, and does exact TIN lookups with no backend, no database, and no network calls. The UI for this lives in [AuditCheckerSection.kt](/Users/marufalam/AndroidStudioProjects/BDTaxCalculator/app/src/main/java/com/maruf/bdtaxcalculator/ui/screen/AuditCheckerSection.kt), and I wired it into the existing main screen in [TaxCalculatorScreen.kt](/Users/marufalam/AndroidStudioProjects/BDTaxCalculator/app/src/main/java/com/maruf/bdtaxcalculator/ui/screen/TaxCalculatorScreen.kt).

It shows real stats from your JSON, supports masked TIN display, distinguishes between selected, not selected, and not found, and exposes zone, circle, category, and assessment year when a record exists. I also added tests for the lookup helpers in [AuditDatasetTest.kt](/Users/marufalam/AndroidStudioProjects/BDTaxCalculator/app/src/test/java/com/maruf/bdtaxcalculator/AuditDatasetTest.kt).

Verification passed with `./gradlew testDebugUnitTest` and `./gradlew assembleDebug`.

One note: the offline dataset you shared currently contains `87,685` records, with `15,489` marked selected and `72,196` not selected, so the UI uses those real numbers instead of the website screenshots.

The UI for this lives in [AuditCheckerSection.kt](/Users/marufalam/AndroidStudioProjects/BDTaxCalculator/app/src/main/java/com/maruf/bdtaxcalculator/ui/screen/AuditCheckerSection.kt), and I wired it into the existing main screen in [TaxCalculatorScreen.kt](/Users/marufalam/AndroidStudioProjects/BDTaxCalculator/app/src/main/java/com/maruf/bdtaxcalculator/ui/screen/TaxCalculatorScreen.kt).

It shows real stats from your JSON, supports masked TIN display, distinguishes between selected, not selected, and not found, and exposes zone, circle, category, and assessment year when a record exists. I also added tests for the lookup helpers in [AuditDatasetTest.kt](/Users/marufalam/AndroidStudioProjects/BDTaxCalculator/app/src/test/java/com/maruf/bdtaxcalculator/AuditDatasetTest.kt).

Verification passed with `./gradlew testDebugUnitTest` and `./gradlew assembleDebug`.

One note: the offline dataset you shared currently contains `87,685` records, with `15,489` marked selected and `72,196` not selected, so the UI uses those real numbers instead of the website screenshots.

I changed the audit checker UI into a more modern, higher-contrast experience in [AuditCheckerSection.kt](/Users/marufalam/AndroidStudioProjects/BDTaxCalculator/app/src/main/java/com/maruf/bdtaxcalculator/ui/screen/AuditCheckerSection.kt). It now has a proper hero card, clearer privacy messaging, a stronger search panel, more polished result states, and a tighter stats layout that feels much closer to a finished product than a stack of default cards.

The main UX upgrades are:

- Result cards that visually separate `selected`, `not selected`, and `not found` states much more clearly.

**Assistant** (2026-04-29 13:13:40):

I added a proper home screen with two service cards and made it the new app entry point. The new flow starts in [HomeScreen.kt](/Users/marufalam/AndroidStudioProjects/BDTaxCalculator/app/src/main/java/com/maruf/bdtaxcalculator/ui/screen/HomeScreen.kt), routes through [AppRootScreen.kt](/Users/marufalam/AndroidStudioProjects/BDTaxCalculator/app/src/main/java/com/maruf/bdtaxcalculator/ui/screen/AppRootScreen.kt), and `MainActivity` now opens that root in [MainActivity.kt](/Users/marufalam/AndroidStudioProjects/BDTaxCalculator/app/src/main/java/com/maruf/bdtaxcalculator/MainActivity.kt).

The two services are now clearly separated in the UX:
- `Tax Calculation` opens the calculator flow directly.
- `Audit Check` opens the offline audit lookup directly.

I also adjusted [TaxCalculatorScreen.kt](/Users/marufalam/AndroidStudioProjects/BDTaxCalculator/app/src/main/java/com/maruf/bdtaxcalculator/ui/screen/TaxCalculatorScreen.kt) so it can work as a dedicated feature screen with back navigation, instead of forcing users through the combined mode switch every time.

I did not split the Gradle project into separate Android modules yet. I intentionally stopped short of that because it’s a bigger structural move with more risk and less immediate UX value. Right now the code is already separated by feature screen and domain package, which gives us a clean base for a later module split if you want.

Verification passed with `./gradlew testDebugUnitTest` and `./gradlew assembleDebug`.

If you want, I can do the next step and reorganize the project into feature-style packages or true Gradle modules like:
1. `feature-home`
2. `feature-tax`
3. `feature-audit`
4. `core-ui` / `core-domain`

**Assistant** (2026-04-29 13:38:57):

I corrected the main calculator-screen mistakes in [TaxCalculatorScreen.kt](/Users/marufalam/AndroidStudioProjects/BDTaxCalculator/app/src/main/java/com/maruf/bdtaxcalculator/ui/screen/TaxCalculatorScreen.kt).

The main fixes were:
- The shared hero is no longer shown above the audit checker when that feature is opened directly from home, which avoids the duplicate-header feel.
- The mode selector layout was corrected so it doesn’t create awkward full-width chip behavior.
- The reusable `SummaryRow` now supports custom colors, so text stays readable on dark cards like the investment recommendation panel.
- `DetailPanel` now actually shows its section title instead of receiving a title and ignoring it.
- The tax slab rows were adjusted so long slab labels don’t collide visually with the tax rate on the right.

I verified the fixes with `./gradlew testDebugUnitTest` and `./gradlew assembleDebug`.

If you want, I can do one more polish pass next on the calculator screen only and make it visually match the home page even more closely.

**Assistant** (2026-04-29 13:38:57):

The main fixes were:
- The shared hero is no longer shown above the audit checker when that feature is opened directly from home, which avoids the duplicate-header feel.
- The mode selector layout was corrected so it doesn’t create awkward full-width chip behavior.
- The reusable `SummaryRow` now supports custom colors, so text stays readable on dark cards like the investment recommendation panel.
- `DetailPanel` now actually shows its section title instead of receiving a title and ignoring it.
- The tax slab rows were adjusted so long slab labels don’t collide visually with the tax rate on the right.

I verified the fixes with `./gradlew testDebugUnitTest` and `./gradlew assembleDebug`.

If you want, I can do one more polish pass next on the calculator screen only and make it visually match the home page even more closely.

Fixed the keyboard issue.

What changed:
- Bottom navigation now hides automatically while the keyboard is open, so it no longer sits above the keyboard and blocks space.
- Money and TIN fields now use `KeyboardType.NumberPassword`, which should reduce extra symbol keys like comma/dot/minus on many Android keyboards.

private fun normalizeNumericInput(input: String, maxLength: Int = MaxMoneyInputLength): String {
    return buildString {
        input.forEach { char ->
            when (char) {
                in '0'..'9' -> append(char)
                in '০'..'৯' -> append(('0'.code + char.code - '০'.code).toChar())
                in '٠'..'٩' -> append(('0'.code + char.code - '٠'.code).toChar())
            }
        }
    }.take(maxLength)
}

**Assistant** (2026-05-01 14:05:44):

Yes, please provide the previous data or Excel sheet. I checked the current code and I already see a few likely causes of wrong results:

- Salary split mismatch: UI says `Basic 60%`, `House 25%`, `Medical 10%`, `Transport 5%`, but code calculates `Basic 50%`, `House 25%`, `Medical 5%`, `Transport 5%`, plus hidden `Other 15%`.
- Result display bug: `TaxResult.totalTax` is currently set to the final tax after rebate, so “মোট কর” and “নিট প্রদেয়” can show the same value.
- Taxpayer limits may need confirmation: FY 2025-26 rates are updated, and women/senior/disabled/freedom-fighter limits may differ from the current constants.
- Excel examples will help confirm whether your app should calculate from gross salary only, or from exact components like basic, house rent, medical, conveyance, bonus, etc.

Please share the Excel or 3-5 sample cases with:
`monthly salary`, `bonus`, `taxpayer type`, `investment`, `expected taxable income`, `expected tax before rebate`, `expected rebate`, `expected final payable`.

I’ll then align the calculator exactly and add unit tests so this doesn’t break again.

Sources checked: [NBR Income Tax Paripatra page](https://nbr.gov.bd/index.php/taxtypes/income-tax/income-tax-paripatra/e-services/vatcalculator/eng), [PwC Bangladesh individual tax summary](https://taxsummaries.pwc.com/bangladesh/individual/taxes-on-personal-income).

**Assistant** (2026-05-01 14:54:22):

Updated the calculator to match the Excel reference.

Key fixes:
- Salary exemption now matches Excel: `min(total salary income / 3, 500,000)`.
- General tax-free slab updated to `350,000`.
- Tax slabs updated to Excel logic: `0%, 5%, 10%, 15%, 20%, 25%, 30%`.
- Minimum tax now applies after rebate like Excel: if calculated tax exists but final is below `5,000`, final payable becomes `5,000`.
- Salary component formula now follows the sheet style: transport `5%`, then basic/house/medical derived from the remaining salary.
- UI labels updated to explain the Excel-based rules more accurately.

I added a unit test matching your Excel sample:
`Gross 50,000`, `Bonus 60,000` gives `Total income 660,000`, `Exemption 220,000`, `Taxable income 440,000`, `Raw tax 4,500`, `Final tax 5,000`.

Verified:
- `./gradlew testDebugUnitTest` passed.
- `./gradlew assembleDebug` passed.

**Assistant** (2026-05-01 15:46:02):

The `১৯,৭০০` result is possible if calculation is done like the old app style:

Example, if income is:

```text
মাসিক বেতন: ৬৫,০০০
বার্ষিক বোনাস: ১৭,০০০
মোট আয়: ৬৫,০০০ × ১২ + ১৭,০০০ = ৭,৯৭,০০০
```

Then slab tax:

```text
প্রথম ৩,৫০,০০০ = ০
পরবর্তী ১,০০,০০০ × ৫% = ৫,০০০
বাকি ৩,৪৭,০০০ × ১০% = ৩৪,৭০০
মোট কর = ৩৯,৭০০
রিবেট/ছাড় = ২০,০০০
প্রদেয় কর = ৩৯,৭০০ - ২০,০০০ = ১৯,৭০০
```

But currently my code calculates differently:

```text
মোট আয় = ৭,৯৭,০০০
বেতন ছাড় = ৭,৯৭,০০০ / ৩ = ২,৬৫,৬৬৬
করযোগ্য আয় = ৭,৯৭,০০০ - ২,৬৫,৬৬৬ = ৫,৩১,৩৩৪
```

Then tax becomes much lower:

```text
প্রথম ৩,৫০,০০০ = ০
পরবর্তী ১,০০,০০০ × ৫% = ৫,০০০
বাকি ৮১,৩৩৪ × ১০% = ৮,১৩৩
মোট কর ≈ ১৩,১৩৩
```

So the mismatch is because I applied `মোট বেতন আয়ের ১/৩ ছাড়`, but your old app/reference seems to calculate tax directly from total yearly income before that exemption. If `১৯,৭০০` is the correct expected result, I should update the app formula to remove that salary exemption step and calculate slab tax on total annual income.

**Assistant** (2026-05-01 15:50:04):

স্ক্রিনশট অনুযায়ী হিসাবটা ঠিক আছে: `৬৫,০০০` মাসিক বেতন + `৭৮,০০০` বার্ষিক বোনাস হলে কর হবে `১৭,২০০`।

হিসাবটা হচ্ছে:

```text
মোট বার্ষিক আয় = ৬৫,০০০ × ১২ + ৭৮,০০০
              = ৮,৫৮,০০০

বেতন ছাড় = ৮,৫৮,০০০ / ৩
         = ২,৮৬,০০০

নিট করযোগ্য আয় = ৮,৫৮,০০০ - ২,৮৬,০০০
               = ৫,৭২,০০০
```

তারপর slab tax:

```text
প্রথম ৩,৫০,০০০ = ০
পরবর্তী ১,০০,০০০ × ৫% = ৫,০০০
বাকি ১,২২,০০০ × ১০% = ১২,২০০

মোট কর = ১৭,২০০
```

আমি এই exact case-এর জন্য unit test add করেছি, যাতে পরে formula ভুলে change না হয়ে যায়। `./gradlew testDebugUnitTest` এবং `./gradlew assembleDebug` দুটোই pass করেছে।

**Assistant** (2026-05-01 16:10:34):

Updated the FY `২০২৫-২৬` tax rules.

Changed:
- General tax-free limit: `৩,৭৫,০০০`
- Female / 65+: `৪,২৫,০০০`
- Third gender / disabled: `৫,০০,০০০`
- Freedom fighter / July fighter: `৫,২৫,০০০`
- Slabs now: `১০%`, `১৫%`, `২০%`, `২৫%`, `৩০%`
- Rebate rule remains lower of: `৩% taxable income`, `১৫% actual investment`, `১০ লাখ`
- Minimum tax: normal `৫,০০০`; new assessment support added as `১,০০০`

Important: with these new rules, `৬৫,০০০` salary + `৭৮,০০০` bonus calculates to `১৯,৭০০`, because taxable income is `৫,৭২,০০০` and `৫,৭২,০০০ - ৩,৭৫,০০০ = ১,৯৭,০০০`, then `১০% = ১৯,৭০০`.

Verified:
- `./gradlew testDebugUnitTest`
- `./gradlew assembleDebug`

## User (2026-05-01 17:50:14)

Since the profile section does not collect any user data, this app is an offline app. So what can be done with the profile section?


**Assistant** (2026-05-01 17:50:36):

For an offline app, the profile section can still be useful, but it should be more like **“Settings / My Tax Setup”** instead of collecting personal user data.

Good options for Profile section:

- **Default Taxpayer Type**: user can choose সাধারণ করদাতা, মহিলা, সিনিয়র, প্রতিবন্ধী, মুক্তিযোদ্ধা. This stays only on device.
- **Default Assessment Type**: regular minimum tax `৫,০০০` or new assessment `১,০০০`.
- **Language / Number Format**: Bangla digits, English digits, or mixed.
- **Privacy Status**: show “Offline app, no server, no database, no tracking of tax inputs.”
- **Data Source Info**: show audit JSON version, tax rule year `২০২৫-২৬`, last app update version.
- **Calculator Preferences**: remember last selected taxpayer type, collapse/expand default, show/hide tips.
- **About App**: app version, disclaimer, NBR rule note.
- **Share App**: share Play Store link.
- **Feedback / Report Issue**: opens email or WhatsApp, but only if user chooses.
- **Clear Local Preferences**: reset saved settings; no tax data stored.

My recommendation: rename **Profile** to **Settings** or **More**. If you still want to keep the label “Profile”, make it a privacy-first local profile: only preferences, no name, phone, email, or login.

Profile section is now redesigned as **সেটিংস** for an offline/privacy-first app.

What changed:
- Removed fake personal profile data like name/email.
- Bottom nav now shows `সেটিংস`.

- Calculator now uses those saved local settings as default.

- `./gradlew assembleDebug`

## User (2026-05-01 19:30:20)

"TaxSlabsCard" is just an info card; is it necessary for TaxCalculatorScreen?


**Assistant** (2026-05-01 19:30:30):

It’s not strictly necessary for `TaxCalculatorScreen`.

Because the calculator already shows:
- selected taxpayer type and tax-free limit
- taxable income
- payable tax
- expandable tax breakdown

`TaxSlabsCard` is mostly educational/reference content. Keeping it can help users trust the calculation, but it also makes the screen longer and slightly more cluttered.

My recommendation: remove it from the main calculator screen and move the rule info into either:

- **Settings** as “কর নিয়ম ২০২৫-২৬”
- **Info dialog** from the toolbar
- **Expandable tax breakdown card** footer, only when user wants details

Best UX for this app: remove `TaxSlabsCard` from `TaxCalculatorScreen`, and keep rule details inside the info button/settings. The calculator screen should stay focused on input → result.

## User (2026-05-02 05:44:32)

now we reday to production playstore ,guide what should i do step by step


**Assistant** (2026-05-02 05:48:40):

You’re close, but production has a few non-code steps. Follow this order.

**1. Decide Privacy Position**
Your app features are mostly offline, but the project currently includes Firebase Analytics, Crashlytics, FCM, Play update checks, `INTERNET`, and notification permission. So in Play Console, do **not** claim “no network / no data collection” unless we remove Firebase/network features.

Recommended production wording:
- Tax/audit input data is processed locally and not stored.
- App may collect diagnostics/analytics/crash data via Firebase if enabled.
- Static audit data is bundled inside the app.

**2. Prepare Required Store Assets**
Already done:
- App icon: `/Users/marufalam/AndroidStudioProjects/BDTaxCalculator/app/src/main/play-store/tax-calculator-bd-icon-512.png`

Still needed:
- Feature graphic: `1024 x 500`, PNG/JPEG, no alpha.
- Minimum 2 phone screenshots, PNG/JPEG, no alpha.
- Short description, long description.
- Privacy policy public URL.
- Contact email.

**3. Create Privacy Policy**
Host it publicly, for example:
- GitHub Pages
- Google Sites
- Your website

Must include:
- Developer/contact info.
- What data is collected by Firebase/Crashlytics/FCM, if kept.
- That tax/TIN inputs are not saved or sent.
- Data retention/deletion info.
- Privacy policy title clearly labeled.

**4. Create Play Console App**
In [Google Play Console](https://play.google.com/console):
1. Create app.
2. App name: `Tax Calculator BD` or your final name.
3. Default language: Bangla or English.
4. App type: App.
5. Free/Paid: choose carefully; paid/free choice has consequences.
6. Category: Finance or Tools, depending your positioning.

**5. Complete App Content**
Go to `Policy and programs > App content` and complete:
- Privacy Policy
- Data Safety
- Ads: likely `No`, unless you added ads.
- App access: likely “All functionality available without special access.”
- Content rating questionnaire.
- Target audience: likely 18+ or general adult finance users.
- News app: No.
- Government app: be careful. Do not imply official NBR/government affiliation unless you have authorization.
- Financial features declaration if Play asks due category/content.

**6. Build Signed Release AAB**
You need a release keystore, not debug signing.

Option A, Android Studio:
1. `Build > Generate Signed Bundle / APK`
2. Select `Android App Bundle`
3. Create/select keystore.
4. Choose `release`
5. Generate `.aab`

Option B, command line after signing config is added:
```bash
./gradlew bundleRelease
```

Output will be:
```text
app/build/outputs/bundle/release/app-release.aab
```

Keep your `.jks` keystore and passwords extremely safe. If lost, future updates become painful.

**7. Upload Internal Test First**
In Play Console:
1. `Test and release > Testing > Internal testing`
2. Create release.
3. Upload signed `.aab`.
4. Add testers’ Gmail accounts.
5. Test install from Play link.
6. Check crash/freezing/keyboard/navigation.

**8. Closed Testing Requirement**
If your Play Developer account is a personal account created after November 13, 2023, Google requires a closed test with at least **12 opted-in testers for 14 continuous days** before production access.

If this applies:
1. Create Closed testing track.
2. Add at least 12 testers.
3. Make sure they opt in and stay opted in for 14 days.
4. Ask them to use calculator, audit check, settings, update flow.
5. Apply for production access.

**9. Production Release**
After all forms pass:
1. `Test and release > Production`
2. Create new release.
3. Upload signed `.aab`
4. Add release notes, for example:
   - Initial release.
   - Offline tax calculator for FY 2025-26.
   - TIN audit check from bundled static data.
5. Run pre-launch report.
6. Start with staged rollout, maybe 10% first.
7. Monitor crashes/reviews.
8. Roll out to 100% if stable.

**10. Before You Submit**
I strongly recommend we do these final checks together:
- Create release signing config safely.
- Generate signed AAB.
- Create feature graphic and screenshots.
- Review privacy policy text.
- Review Data Safety answers based on Firebase/network choice.
- Check app name/package/version.

Official docs:
- [App signing](https://developer.android.com/guide/publishing/app-signing.html)
- [Prepare and roll out a release](https://support.google.com/googleplay/android-developer/answer/9859348)
- [Data Safety form](https://support.google.com/googleplay/android-developer/answer/10787469)
- [Testing requirements](https://support.google.com/googleplay/android-developer/answer/14151465)
- [Store listing assets](https://support.google.com/googleplay/android-developer/answer/9866151)

**6. Build Signed Release AAB**
You need a release keystore, not debug signing.

Option A, Android Studio:
1. `Build > Generate Signed Bundle / APK`
2. Select `Android App Bundle`
3. Create/select keystore.
4. Choose `release`
5. Generate `.aab`

Option B, command line after signing config is added:
```bash
./gradlew bundleRelease
```

Output will be:
```text
app/build/outputs/bundle/release/app-release.aab
```

Keep your `.jks` keystore and passwords extremely safe. If lost, future updates become painful.

**7. Upload Internal Test First**
In Play Console:
1. `Test and release > Testing > Internal testing`
2. Create release.
3. Upload signed `.aab`.
4. Add testers’ Gmail accounts.
5. Test install from Play link.
6. Check crash/freezing/keyboard/navigation.

**8. Closed Testing Requirement**
If your Play Developer account is a personal account created after November 13, 2023, Google requires a closed test with at least **12 opted-in testers for 14 continuous days** before production access.

If this applies:
1. Create Closed testing track.
2. Add at least 12 testers.
3. Make sure they opt in and stay opted in for 14 days.
4. Ask them to use calculator, audit check, settings, update flow.
5. Apply for production access.

**9. Production Release**
After all forms pass:
1. `Test and release > Production`
2. Create new release.
3. Upload signed `.aab`
4. Add release notes, for example:
   - Initial release.
   - Offline tax calculator for FY 2025-26.
   - TIN audit check from bundled static data.
5. Run pre-launch report.
6. Start with staged rollout, maybe 10% first.
7. Monitor crashes/reviews.
8. Roll out to 100% if stable.

**10. Before You Submit**
I strongly recommend we do these final checks together:
- Create release signing config safely.
- Generate signed AAB.
- Create feature graphic and screenshots.
- Review privacy policy text.
- Review Data Safety answers based on Firebase/network choice.
- Check app name/package/version.

Official docs:
- [App signing](https://developer.android.com/guide/publishing/app-signing.html)
- [Prepare and roll out a release](https://support.google.com/googleplay/android-developer/answer/9859348)
- [Data Safety form](https://support.google.com/googleplay/android-developer/answer/10787469)
- [Testing requirements](https://support.google.com/googleplay/android-developer/answer/14151465)
- [Store listing assets](https://support.google.com/googleplay/android-developer/answer/9866151)

**Assistant** (2026-05-02 06:23:23):

This issue is Play Console form incomplete. Your release bundle already contains Advertising ID permissions through Firebase/Google SDK:

```text
com.google.android.gms.permission.AD_ID
android.permission.ACCESS_ADSERVICES_AD_ID
```

So in Play Console choose **Yes**.

Steps:

1. Go to **Play Console**
2. Open your app
3. Go to **Policy and programs > App content**
4. Find **Advertising ID**
5. Click **Start / Manage**
6. Select:

```text
Does your app use advertising ID?
Yes
```

7. Purpose:

```text
Analytics
```

8. Do **not** select Ads/Marketing unless you use AdMob or ads.
9. Save.
10. Go back to release page and check again.

Reason: your app uses Firebase Analytics/Crashlytics/FCM, and Firebase/Google Play services can include Advertising ID permission. Since target SDK is Android 13+, Google requires this declaration.

Official ref: [Google Play Advertising ID policy](https://support.google.com/googleplay/android-developer/answer/6048248)

## User (2026-05-02 06:41:11)

Data safety need any change?


**Assistant** (2026-05-02 06:41:41):

Yes, you should update Data Safety because this app now includes Firebase Analytics, Crashlytics, FCM, Advertising ID permission, and notification/update services.

In Play Console Data Safety, don’t say “No data collected.” Use this safer setup:

**Data Collection**
Answer: `Yes, this app collects or shares user data`

**Data Types To Declare**
- `Device or other IDs`
  Purpose: `Analytics`, `App functionality`, `Fraud prevention, security, and compliance`
- `App info and performance`
  Include: `Crash logs`, `Diagnostics`, maybe `Other app performance data`
  Purpose: `Analytics`, `App functionality`
- `App activity`
  Include: `App interactions`
  Purpose: `Analytics`
- `Device info`
  If shown, include device/app metadata for diagnostics.

**Data Sharing**
Usually choose `No` for sharing if you only use Firebase as a service provider and do not sell/share data separately. But Google/Firebase processing still must be disclosed as collected.

**Security Practices**
- `Data is encrypted in transit`: `Yes`
- `Users can request data deletion`: choose based on your privacy policy. If you don’t have account/user data, mention that tax/TIN inputs are not stored; Firebase analytics/crash data follows Firebase/Google retention.
- `Data collection optional`: likely `No`, unless you add an opt-out toggle for Analytics/Crashlytics.

**Important note for your app**
Tax calculator inputs and TIN search values are not saved or sent by your app, so do **not** mark financial info or personal info for those user-entered values unless you later transmit/store them.

Also update your privacy policy to mention:
- Firebase Analytics
- Firebase Crashlytics
- Firebase Cloud Messaging
- Advertising ID used for analytics/measurement if available
- Tax/TIN input remains local and is not stored by your app

Official Firebase disclosure helper: [Firebase Data Safety guide](https://firebase.google.com/docs/android/play-data-disclosure)

**Assistant** (2026-05-02 16:48:01):

Yes, send it from **Firebase Console > Messaging / Cloud Messaging**.

Use this message:

```text
Title:
নতুন আপডেট এসেছে

Body:
Tax Calculator BD 1.0.6 এখন Play Store-এ পাওয়া যাচ্ছে। নতুন কর নিয়ম, উন্নত UI ও স্থিতিশীলতার জন্য অ্যাপটি আপডেট করুন।
```

Steps:

1. Open [Firebase Console](https://console.firebase.google.com/)
2. Select your project for `com.maruf.bdtaxcalculator`
3. Go to **Messaging** or **Cloud Messaging**
4. Click **Create your first campaign** / **New notification**
5. Choose **Firebase Notification messages**
6. Add title/body from above
7. Target:
   - App: `BD Tax Calculator`
   - Platform: Android
   - If option available, target app version **less than 1.0.6**
   - Otherwise send to all app users
8. Schedule: **Now**
9. Review and Publish

Important: notification tap will open your app. Since we already added Play in-app update, users should see the update flow after opening the app.

Small warning: only users whose installed app version already has FCM + notification permission can receive this push. Users who denied notification permission may not see it.

Firebase docs:
- [Send messages with Firebase console](https://firebase.google.com/docs/cloud-messaging/android/send-with-console)
- [FCM targeting options](https://firebase.google.com/docs/cloud-messaging)

**Assistant** (2026-05-05 18:08:20):

Implemented localization + day/night mode foundation.

What’s added:
- Language setting: `বাংলা` / `English`
- Theme setting: `System`, `Light`, `Dark`
- Preferences saved locally with `SharedPreferences`
- App theme updates immediately without restart
- Settings screen and bottom navigation now switch Bangla/English
- Added dark Material color scheme and app-level dark mode support

Verified:
- `./gradlew testDebugUnitTest`
- `./gradlew assembleDebug`

Note: most calculator/audit content is still hardcoded Bangla, so I added the localization system and migrated the app shell/settings first. We can now progressively move calculator, audit, and home text into the same localization pattern.

**Assistant** (2026-05-05 19:17:41):

Implemented seamless Android In-App Review flow.

What changed:
- Added Play Review dependencies: `review` and `review-ktx` `2.0.2`.
- Added `InAppReviewManager` with local throttling, version guard, cooldown, and Firebase event logging.
- Triggers review only after meaningful usage: tax calculation completion or audit search completion.
- Settings “Rate app” now opens the Play Store listing as a reliable manual fallback.
- Kept flow silent on errors, matching Google guidance.

Verified:
- `./gradlew assembleDebug` passed
- `./gradlew testDebugUnitTest` passed
- `./gradlew bundleRelease` passed
- Release AAB generated at `app/build/outputs/bundle/release/app-release.aab`

Reference used: [Android In-App Review guide](https://developer.android.com/guide/playcore/in-app-review/kotlin-java?hl=en)

কাজগুলো:
- Home bell icon এখন clickable, unread badge দেখায়।
- Bell চাপলে bottom sheet notification inbox খুলবে।
- Firebase FCM message এলে system notification দেখানোর পাশাপাশি app-এর local inbox-এ save হবে।
- Clear action added।
- Read/unread state local device-এ থাকে, কোনো backend/database নেই।

- [TaxProMessagingService.kt](/Users/marufalam/AndroidStudioProjects/BDTaxCalculator/app/src/main/java/com/maruf/bdtaxcalculator/firebase/TaxProMessagingService.kt)
- [HomeScreen.kt](/Users/marufalam/AndroidStudioProjects/BDTaxCalculator/app/src/main/java/com/maruf/bdtaxcalculator/ui/screen/HomeScreen.kt)

Important note: Firebase `data` payload notification দিলে inbox-এ reliably save হবে। Pure `notification-only` push background-এ Android নিজে show করতে পারে, তখন app service সবসময় receive নাও করতে পারে। For best result, Firebase থেকে `title` + `body` data payload সহ notification পাঠাবেন।

**Assistant** (2026-05-07 06:40:14):

এটা জানতে সবচেয়ে clean way হলো **Firebase Analytics event** log করা, যখন user dark mode-এর appearance color change করে।

Example event:
```kotlin
FirebaseTracker.logEvent(
    "dark_theme_palette_selected",
    Bundle().apply {
        putString("palette", selectedPalette.name)
        putString("theme_mode", "dark")
    }
)
```

তারপর Firebase Console-এ দেখতে পারবেন কোনটা বেশি popular:
`Analytics` → `Events` → `dark_theme_palette_selected` → parameter `palette`

আমার suggestion:
- User যখন `Olive Night`, `Classic Green`, `Midnight Black` select করবে, তখন event log করবো।
- শুধু dark mode selected থাকলে এই event যাবে।
- কোনো personal data যাবে না, শুধু theme choice যাবে।

Important privacy note: যেহেতু এটা Firebase Analytics-এ যাবে, app আর strictly “fully offline analytics-free” থাকবে না। কিন্তু personal info ছাড়া শুধুমাত্র preference tracking হিসেবে এটা safe and Play Store Data Safety-তে already analytics-related disclosure থাকলে okay।

private fun normalizeNumericInput(input: String, maxLength: Int = MaxMoneyInputLength): String {
    return buildString {
        input.forEach { char ->
            when (char) {
                in '0'..'9' -> append(char)
                in '০'..'৯' -> append(('0'.code + char.code - '০'.code).toChar())
                in '٠'..'٩' -> append(('0'.code + char.code - '٠'.code).toChar())
            }
        }
    }.take(maxLength)
}

Implemented the Android best-practice foundation for runtime language switching.

What changed:
- Added Android per-app locale support with `AppCompatDelegate.setApplicationLocales()`.
- Enabled AGP generated locale config for Android 13+ app language settings.
- Added supported locale filters: `en`, `bn`.
- Added English default resources and Bangla `values-bn/strings.xml`.
- Added `AppLocaleManager`.
- MainActivity now applies selected language through Android locale APIs, not only custom Compose state.

Key files:
- [AppLocaleManager.kt](/Users/marufalam/AndroidStudioProjects/BDTaxCalculator/app/src/main/java/com/maruf/bdtaxcalculator/ui/AppLocaleManager.kt)
- [MainActivity.kt](/Users/marufalam/AndroidStudioProjects/BDTaxCalculator/app/src/main/java/com/maruf/bdtaxcalculator/MainActivity.kt)
- [strings.xml](/Users/marufalam/AndroidStudioProjects/BDTaxCalculator/app/src/main/res/values/strings.xml)

Verified:
- `./gradlew assembleDebug` passed
- `./gradlew testDebugUnitTest` passed

Important: foundation is now correct. For 100% Bangla/English coverage, remaining hardcoded Compose texts should gradually move into `strings.xml` / `values-bn/strings.xml` and use `stringResource()`. This follows Google’s recommended per-app language approach: [Android per-app language preferences](https://developer.android.com/guide/topics/resources/app-languages).

**Assistant** (2026-05-07 08:30:56):

Implemented English/Bangla runtime language polish and English font switching.

What changed:
- English mode now uses **Quicksand** automatically.
- Bangla mode keeps **Tiro Bangla**.
- Home, Tax Calculator, Audit, Settings visible labels/messages are now language-aware.
- Numbers/percentages now switch digits by language: English digits in English mode, Bangla digits in Bangla mode.
- Tax slab labels also switch between English and Bangla.
- App language infrastructure remains Android best-practice per-app locale based.

Verified:
- `./gradlew assembleDebug` passed
- `./gradlew testDebugUnitTest` passed

One note: Firebase notification title/body and audit dataset values will display exactly what the source data sends. If you want those fully bilingual too, Firebase payload/audit data should include both English and Bangla versions.

**Assistant** (2026-05-07 08:30:56):

What changed:
- English mode now uses **Quicksand** automatically.
- Bangla mode keeps **Tiro Bangla**.

- Numbers/percentages now switch digits by language: English digits in English mode, Bangla digits in Bangla mode.
- Tax slab labels also switch between English and Bangla.
- App language infrastructure remains Android best-practice per-app locale based.

Verified:
- `./gradlew assembleDebug` passed
- `./gradlew testDebugUnitTest` passed

One note: Firebase notification title/body and audit dataset values will display exactly what the source data sends. If you want those fully bilingual too, Firebase payload/audit data should include both English and Bangla versions.

**Force Update**
App-side force update is already workable. `PlayStoreUpdateManager` uses Google Play In-App Updates and triggers `IMMEDIATE` update when Play update priority is `4` or higher.

For force update:
1. Upload `app-release.aab` to Play Console production.
2. If Play Console shows native symbol warning, upload `native-debug-symbols-release.zip`.
3. To force update existing users, set in-app update priority to `5` when publishing via Google Play Developer API, or use Play Console recovery tool: “Prompt users to update” after the release goes live.
4. If priority is not set, app will fallback to soft/flexible update.

**Release Notes**
```text
New in version 1.0.7:
- Added Bangla and English language support.
- Added Quicksand font for English and Tiro Bangla for Bangla.
- Added day/night theme support with selectable dark appearances.
- Added Firebase notification inbox on Home.
- Improved tax calculator UI, keyboard behavior, and expand/collapse animations.
- Added Firebase Analytics, Crashlytics, FCM, and in-app review support.
- Improved Play Store update flow with soft/force update support.
- Performance, UI polish, and bug fixes.
```

Google rating/in-app review check: the code is wired correctly through `ReviewManager`, and the Settings “Rate app” button opens the Play Store listing. The actual Google review dialog only appears when installed from Play/Internal Testing and Google allows it by quota, so it cannot be fully forced from local debug.

**Assistant** (2026-05-07 11:31:05):

Custom soft/force update UI added.

How it works:
- Soft update: shows custom dialog with `Maybe Later` and `Update Now`.
- Force update: shows required update UI with no outside/back dismiss and only `Get latest version`.
- If Play update priority is `4` or `5`, app shows force update UI and starts `IMMEDIATE` update.
- If priority is lower, app shows soft update UI and starts `FLEXIBLE` update.
- UI supports Bangla/English and app theme/font automatically.

**Assistant** (2026-05-07 11:54:54):

আপনার screenshot অনুযায়ী এখন `7 (1.0.7)` এখনো `Draft`, তাই tester device এখনো update পাবে না. Real Play Store update দেখতে হলে:

1. Tester phone-এ Play Store থেকে current internal version `2 (1.0.1)` install করুন।
2. App একবার open করে Settings-এ version confirm করুন।
3. Play Console-এ draft `7 (1.0.7)` release করুন: `Review release` → `Roll out to internal testing`.
4. Manually Play Store থেকে update চাপবেন না। Old app open করুন।
5. App start হলে আমাদের `PlayStoreUpdateManager` update check করবে এবং custom update prompt দেখাবে।

Force update note: আমাদের code priority `4+` হলে force update দেখায়। Google docs অনুযায়ী `inAppUpdatePriority` release করার সময় Play Developer API দিয়ে set করতে হয়; পরে change করা যায় না. Priority না দিলে usually soft update দেখবে।

In-app review দেখতে:
1. Tester Google account Internal testing list-এ থাকতে হবে।
2. Same account Play Store-এর primary account হতে হবে।
3. App অবশ্যই Play Store/Internal testing থেকে install করা হতে হবে, Android Studio/debug install না।
4. Account-এ আগে review দেওয়া থাকলে review sheet নাও আসতে পারে।
5. App 2 বার open করুন, তারপর Tax calculate বা Audit search মোট 3 বার complete করুন। তখন review request trigger হবে।

Important: Google in-app review UI quota-based, তাই request successful হলেও sheet সবসময় দেখাবে না. Settings-এর `Rate app` button review sheet না, এটা সরাসরি Play Store listing খোলে।

Official refs: [Test in-app updates](https://developer.android.com/guide/playcore/in-app-updates/test), [Test in-app reviews](https://developer.android.com/guide/playcore/in-app-review/test), [Update priority](https://developer.android.com/guide/playcore/in-app-updates/kotlin-java).

- Updated privacy text in Settings to clarify: app does not store TIN/payment data; the official portal loads only when user opens it.
- I did not use the hardcoded curl token/cookie because those are session-based and unsafe to ship.

- `./gradlew assembleDebug` passed
- `./gradlew testDebugUnitTest` passed
- `./gradlew bundleRelease` passed

Release AAB:
`/Users/marufalam/AndroidStudioProjects/BDTaxCalculator/app/build/outputs/bundle/release/app-release.aab`

**Assistant** (2026-06-06 15:47:51):

Implemented exactly that flow: user enters 12-digit TIN, app calls the official NBR-Sonali endpoint, parses the returned HTML, and shows Name, Address, Tax Zone, Tax Circle, Account Code, and TIN type inside the app.

Key files:
- [NbrTinLookupRepository.kt](/Users/marufalam/AndroidStudioProjects/BDTaxCalculator/app/src/main/java/com/maruf/bdtaxcalculator/nbr/NbrTinLookupRepository.kt): GET fresh token/cookie, POST TIN, parse result.
- [NbrTinCheckScreen.kt](/Users/marufalam/AndroidStudioProjects/BDTaxCalculator/app/src/main/java/com/maruf/bdtaxcalculator/ui/screen/NbrTinCheckScreen.kt): native input/result UI, no WebView.
- No hardcoded token/cookie, no TIN/result saved, no TIN sent to Firebase Analytics.

I also verified the real NBR POST request format works: response returned populated fields for Name/Address/Zone/Circle/Account Code.

Build verified:
- `./gradlew assembleDebug` passed
- `./gradlew testDebugUnitTest` passed
- `./gradlew bundleRelease` passed

Release AAB:
`/Users/marufalam/AndroidStudioProjects/BDTaxCalculator/app/build/outputs/bundle/release/app-release.aab`

One important Play Store note: this feature sends user-entered TIN to the official NBR-Sonali website, so the app is no longer fully offline for this feature. Data Safety / privacy text should mention this network lookup.

## User (2026-06-06 17:46:09)

The government has proposed no increase in the annual tax-free income limit for individual taxpayers in the national budget for the 2025–26 fiscal year, despite ongoing high inflation.

In the budget, finance adviser Salehuddin Ahmed announced that the tax-free income threshold for individual taxpayers would remain unchanged at Tk 350,000. However, a new tax slab has been proposed at the upper end of the income scale, that will be subjected to a 30 per cent tax.

Under the revised structure, the first Tk 350,000 of income will remain tax-free. The next Tk 100,000 will be taxed at 5 per cent, followed by 10 per cent on the subsequent Tk 400,000, 15 per cent on the next Tk 500,000, 20 per cent on next Tk 500,000, and 25 per cent on next Tk 2 million. As per the newly proposed income slab, any income above Tk 3.85 million will be taxed at the rate of 30 per cent.

The budget also proposed an increase in the tax-free income threshold for the 2026–27 and 2027–28 fiscal years. In both of those years, the limit will be raised by Tk 25,000, bringing the tax-free ceiling to Tk 375,000.

For women and senior citizens aged 65 or above, the tax-free income threshold is Tk 400,000. For third-gender individuals and persons with disabilities, the limit is Tk 475,000. Gazetted freedom fighters are allowed a higher threshold of Tk 500,000.

Additionally, parents or legal guardians of persons with disabilities will receive an extra Tk 50,000 tax-free allowance per dependent.

The government also proposed that from the 2026–27 fiscal year, individuals injured during the July uprising who are officially recognised will be entitled to a tax-free income threshold of Tk 525,000.

Tk 1,000 minimum tax for new taxpayers
For new taxpayers, the finance adviser proposed a minimum tax of Tk 1,000 once their income exceeds the tax-free limit. Currently, minimum tax rates vary based on the taxpayer’s location. Those residing in Dhaka North, Dhaka South, and Chattogram City Corporation areas must pay Tk 5,000. In other city corporations, the minimum tax is Tk 4,000, while taxpayers outside city corporation areas pay Tk 3,000.

According to data from the National Board of Revenue (NBR), Bangladesh currently has around 11.1 million registered taxpayers with Taxpayer Identification Numbers (TINs). Of them, approximately 4 million individuals submitted tax returns during the current fiscal year.

    return minOf(rebateByInvestment, rebateByIncome, TaxDefaults.maxInvestmentRebate)
}
